This protein appears to be an SWEET sugar transporter (based on the transmembrane topology and the presence of the conserved motifs). SWEET proteins are 7TM repeat × 2 proteins that transport sugars including glucose.

In SWEET transporters, the sugar-binding residues are typically located in the middle of the transmembrane helices, facing the pore. The conserved residues in the binding cavity include asparagines and glutamines that form hydrogen bonds with the sugar, and sometimes aromatic residues.

Looking at the literature on SWEET transporters, the D-glucose binding residues typically include:
- Glutamine (Q) residues
- Asparagine (N) residues  
- Sometimes aromatic residues like tryptophan (W)

In Arabidopsis SWEET1 (AtSWEET1), which transports glucose, the binding residues include:

- N at position 321
- N at position 352
- W at position 427
